L1422: The demographics and baseline disease characteristics were similar across different patient population subgroups in the corresponding treatment group and exposure quartiles, indicating the dropouts were not demographics and baseline disease characteristics dependent. However, patients with lower exposure in 300 mg treatment group tend to have a higher bodyweight at baseline, as bodyweight is positively correlated with anifrolumab clearance. Also, patients with lower exposure in 300 mg treatment group tend to have a higher type I IFN gene signature, SLEDAI, and oral corticosteroids dose at baseline. Due to these unbalanced disease characteristics at baseline, the positive exposure response relationship with in 300 mg treatment group (as shown in grey color in all the following analysis) unnecessarily indicated a positive exposure response relationship below or beyond the observed exposure range in 300 mg Q4W dosing regimen.
L1423: ## 16.4.3.2. BICLA
L1424: ## BICLA in High IFN Completer
L1425: The Applicant conducted a logistic regression analysis of the BICLA response rate by evaluating the significance of a continuous Cave in type I IFN test high patients who completed treatment (150 mg and 300 mg). The analysis results are depicted in Figure 32, and the parameter estimates are listed in Table 83.

L1538: ## 1 Executive Summary
L1539: ## 1.1 Introduction
L1540: AstraZeneca submitted a 351(a) Biologics License Application (BLA) on July 22, 2020, for Saphnelo™ (anifrolumab-fnia, MEDI-546) a proposed type I interferon receptor subunit 1 (IFNAR1) antagonist for the treatment of adult patients with moderate to severe systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE), (b) (4) This is a review of the nonclinical sections of the Applicant's proposed labeling received on July 31, 2020 (Supporting Document Number [SDN] 1). This review evaluated the Applicant's proposed Indications and Usage section under Highlights of Prescribing Information and the following sections under Full Prescribing Information: Section 8.1 (Pregnancy), Section 8.2 (Lactation), Section 11 (Description; first paragraph only), Section 12.1 (Mechanism of Action), and Section 13 (Nonclinical Toxicology). An integrated review and evaluation of the nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology studies to support the safety of anifrolumab-fnia for approval was completed on March 31, 2021 and is included in the BLA multidisciplinary review and evaluation document (i.e., the unireview).
L1541: ## 1.3 Recommendations
L1542: ## 1.3.3 Labeling
L1543: Provided below are the recommended nonclinical changes to the sections of the Applicant's proposed prescribing information mentioned in the **Introduction**.
L1544: The underlined text is recommended for insertion and the strikethrough text is recommended for deletion.
L1545: ## HIGHLIGHTS OF PRESCRIBING INFORMATION
L1546: ## INDICATIONS AND USAGE
L1547: (b) (4) SAPHNELO is a type I interferon (IFN) receptor (b) (4) antagonist indicated for the treatment of adult patients with moderate to severe systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE), (b) (4) (1)
L1548: ## FULL PRESCRIBING INFORMATION
L1549: ## 8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS
L1550: ## 8.1 Pregnancy
L1551: ## Pregnancy Exposure Registry
L1552: ## Risk Summary
L1553: (b) (4) limited (b) (4) human data (b) (4) with (b) (4) are insufficient to inform on drug-associated risk for major birth defects, miscarriage, or adverse maternal or fetal outcome. Monoclonal IgG antibodies are (b) (4) known to be actively transported across the placenta as pregnancy progresses; therefore,
L1554: anifrolumab-fnia exposure to the fetus may be greater during the third trimester of pregnancy.
L1555: In an enhanced pre- and post-natal (b) (4) development study (b) (4) with pregnant cynomolgus monkeys that received intravenous administration of anifrolumab-fnia, there was no evidence of (b) (4) embryotoxicity or fetal malformations with exposures up to approximately (b) (4) 28 times the exposure at the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD) (b) (4) on an Area Under Curve (AUC) basis [(see *Data*)].
L1556: All pregnancies have a background risk of birth defect, loss, or other adverse outcomes. The estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage for the indicated population is unknown. In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2% to 4% and 15% to 20%, respectively.
L1557: ## Data
L1558: ***Animal Data:*** In an enhanced pre- and post-natal (b) (4) development study, pregnant cynomolgus monkeys received anifrolumab-fnia (b) (4) at (b) (4) intravenous doses of 30 or 60 mg/kg once every 2 weeks from confirmation of pregnancy at Gestation Day 20, throughout the gestation period, and continuing until 1-month post-partum (approximately Lactation Day 28). There was no evidence of anifrolumab-fnia related maternal toxicity, embryo-fetal toxicity, or post-natal developmental effects. No anifrolumab-fnia related effect on T-cell-dependent antibody response in the infants was noted up to Day 180 after birth. The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) for maternal and developmental toxicity was identified as 60 mg/kg ((b) (4) approximately (b) (4) 28 times the MRHD (b) (4) on an AUC basis). In the infants, mean serum concentrations of anifrolumab-fnia on Day 30 after birth increased with dose and were approximately 4.2% to 9.7% of the respective maternal concentrations. The anifrolumab-fnia concentrations in the infant serum were up to approximately 22 times the concentrations in the maternal milk, suggesting that anifrolumab-fnia had transferred via the placenta.
L1559: ## 8.2 Lactation
L1560: ## Risk Summary
L1561: No data are available regarding the presence of (b) (4) SAPHNELO in human milk, the effects on the breastfed child, or the effects on milk production.
L1562: Anifrolumab-fnia (b) (4) was detected in the milk of female cynomolgus monkeys administered anifrolumab-fnia (b) (4)
L1563: Due to species-species differences in lactation physiology, animal data may not reliably predict drug levels in humans. Maternal IgG is known to be present in human milk. If anifrolumab-fnia is transferred into human milk, the effects of local gastrointestinal exposure and limited systemic exposure in the breastfed infant to anifrolumab-fnia are unknown.
L1564: The developmental and health benefits of breastfeeding should be considered along with the mother's clinical need for anifrolumab-fnia(b)(4) and any potential adverse effects on the breast-fed child from anifrolumab-fnia(b)(4) or from the underlying maternal condition.
L1565: ## 11 DESCRIPTION
L1566: Anifrolumab-fnia(b)(4) is a type I interferon (IFN) receptor antagonist(b)(4), immunoglobulin G1 kappa (IgG1k) monoclonal antibody that(b)(4) is produced in mouse myeloma cells (NS0) by recombinant DNA technology. The molecular weight is approximately 148 kDa.
L1567: SAPHNELO(b)(4) (anifrolumab-fnia(b)(4)) injection is a sterile, preservative-free, clear to opalescent, colorless to slightly yellow, solution for intravenous use. SAPHNELO contains anifrolumab-fnia at a concentration of 150 mg/mL in a single-dose vial.
L1568: (b)(4) Each vial contains 300 mg (150 mg/mL) of anifrolumab-fnia(b)(4), L-histidine (3 mg), L-histidine hydrochloride monohydrate (6 mg), L-lysine hydrochloride (18 mg), polysorbate 80 (1 mg), trehalose dihydrate (98 mg), polysorbate 80 (1 mg) and Water for injection, USP. The pH is 5.9.
L1569: ## 12 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
L1570: ## 12.1 Mechanism of Action
L1571: Anifrolumab-fnia(b)(4) is a human IgG1k monoclonal antibody that binds to subunit 1 of the type I interferon receptor (IFNAR)(b)(4) with high specificity and affinity. This binding inhibits type I IFN signaling, thereby blocking the biologic activity of type I IFNs. Anifrolumab-fnia(b)(4) also induces the internalization of IFNAR1, thereby reducing the levels of cell surface IFNAR1 available for receptor assembly. Blockade of receptor mediated type I IFN signaling inhibits IFN responsive gene expression as well as downstream inflammatory and immunological processes. Inhibition of type I IFN blocks plasma cell differentiation and normalized peripheral T-cell subsets,(b)(4)
L1572: Type I IFNs play an important role in the pathogenesis of SLE.(b)(4) Approximately 60-80% adult patients with active SLE express elevated levels of type I IFN inducible genes,(b)(4)
L1573: ## 13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
L1574: ## 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility
L1575: The carcinogenic and genotoxic potential of anifrolumab-fnia(b)(4) have not been evaluated. In rodent models of IFNAR1 blockade, increased carcinogenic potential has been observed. The clinical relevance of these findings is unknown.
L1576: Effects on male and female fertility have not been directly evaluated in animal studies. No anifrolumab-fnia-related adverse effects on indirect measures of male or female fertility, based on semen analysis, spermatogenesis staging, menses cycle, organ weights and histopathological findings in the reproductive organs were observed in 9-month repeat-dose toxicity studies in cynomolgus monkeys at doses up to 50 mg/kg intravenous(b)(4) once weekly (approximately(b)(4) 58 times the MRHD on an AUC basis).
L1577: ## 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L1578: Presented above is the Applicant's proposed draft labeling text for the SAPHNELO (anifrolumab-fnia) prescribing information dated July 31, 2020 (SDN 1) and the nonclinical revisions to the Applicant's proposed labeling. The underlined text is recommended for insertion and the strikethrough text is recommended for deletion from the Applicant's proposed text. Below are rationales for the proposed changes for each section.
L1579: ## INDICATIONS AND USAGE
L1580: SAPHNELO is a human IgG1κ that binds to IFNAR1. Since this is a first-in-class product targeting IFNAR, the Applicant proposed "type I interferon (IFN) receptor-(b)(4)" as the established pharmacological class (EPC) for anifrolumab. The relevant pharmacology data as listed below were reviewed by Dr. Luqi Pei on 01/18/2011 under IND 101849.
L1581: - Anifrolumab has high affinity for IFNAR1 expressed on human and cynomolgus monkey peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) with KD of 0.29 and 0.65 nM, respectively (study# RIA546-0002).
- Anifrolumab bound to the sub-domain 3 of human IFNAR1 (265-307) with residues 276-279 identified as the critical epitope (study# RIA546-0004).
- Anifrolumab competitively inhibited the binding of IFN-α-2a to IFNAR in Daudi cells (study# RIA546-0003).
- Anifrolumab inhibitedIFN-stimulated response elements (ISRE) activation induced by all the recombinant human IFN-α subtypes (study# RIA546-0006) and SLE sera (study# RIA546-0008),
- expression of Type-I IFN responsive genes (USP18, HERC5/6, IFIT1/3/5, G1P2/3, MX1/2, OAS1/2, IFI44, IFI44L, Ly6E) from PBMCs by sera of SLE patients (study# RIA546-0005),
- expression of differentiation markers (CD123 and CD38) and cytokines/chemokines (IL-6, IL-10, MCP-1, MCP-2) from monocytes by leukocyte-IFNs (study# RIA546-0005).
L1582: These data suggest that anifrolumab is specifically targeting IFNAR1 and inhibiting type I IFN signaling, thereby blocking the biologic activity of type I IFNs. Our internal EPC
L1583: assessment, which included a request by email on 01/12/2021 to Dr. Paul Brown of the Office of New Drug Immediate Office for an evaluation of the EPC text phrase, also determined that,
L1584: - It may be best to not include (b) (4) in the EPC so the term focuses on the pharmacology rather than (b) (4);
- (b) (4) Since anifrolumab is binding to the IFNAR1 receptor and preventing activity there, it is more appropriate to be classified as an antagonist.
L1585: Therefore, “type I IFN receptor antagonist” is recommended as the EPC for SAPHNELO.
L1586: ## 8.1 Pregnancy and 8.2 Lactation
L1587: General edits were made to Sections 8.1 and 8.2 to remove unnecessary text. The post marketing requirement (PMR) for pregnancy and lactation, including Pregnancy Registry was recommended by the Division of Pediatrics and Maternal Health (DPMH) on 5/24/2021. The Applicant agreed with the recommendations on 07/26/2021 (SDN 47). The language for Pregnancy Exposure Registry was added to Section 8.1 (not shown in this review because it is not relevant to nonclinical labeling text).
L1588: For Risk Summary in Section 8.1, the Applicant estimated that the exposures in pregnant monkeys without maternal and developmental toxicity was up to approximately (b) (4) times the exposure at the MRHD on an AUC basis. Since the AUC_{0-14d} for the NOAEL of 60 mg/kg once every 2 weeks was 20900 \mug.d/mL, and the highest mean AUC_{0-28d} in patients with SLE receiving repeat IV dosing of 300 mg anifrolumab-fnia (once every 4 weeks for 52 weeks) was 1472 \mug.d/mL between Weeks 48 to 52 for low Type 1 IFN gene signature subjects, the safety margin was calculated to be 28 (= 20900 x 2/1472). A factor of 2 is applied to match the duration of AUC values before the comparison, i.e., 14 days vs. 28 days.
L1589: For Animal Data in Section 8.1, language was added to indicate that there is no anifrolumab-fnia related effect on T-cell-dependent antibody response in the infants up to Day 180 after birth. Additional language was added to indicate the potential of placenta transfer of anifrolumab-fnia based on the detection of anifrolumab-fnia in the serum of neonates up to one month after birth.
L1590: For Risk Summary in Section 8.2, additional standard language was added. Although anifrolumab-fnia was detected in the milk of female cynomolgus monkeys, we added that “due to species-species differences in lactation physiology, animal data may not reliably predict drug levels in humans”.
L1591: ## 11 DESCRIPTION
L1592: Revisions to the first paragraph of Section 11 were made by nonclinical. The term “type I IFN receptor antagonist” was used to be consistent with the recommend EPC text
L1593: phrase in the Indications and Usage section under Highlights of Prescribing Information. The description (b)(4) was deleted (b)(4). Molecular weight was added.
L1594: Edits in the remaining paragraphs of Section 11 were made by the Office of Biotechnology Products (OBP).
L1595: ## 12 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
L1596: ## 12.1 Mechanism of Action
L1597: Revisions to Section 12.1 were made to remove any potential promotional language.
L1598: ## 13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
L1599: ## 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility
L1600: Blocking the IFNAR1 pathway is expected to have increased carcinogenic potential. In 2014, the Applicant proposed to address the carcinogenic potential by product labeling and clinical risk management practices without performing additional in vivo animal studies. The agency agreed with their plan on 10/15/2014 under IND 101849 (End of Phase 2 meeting minutes). The Applicant also reiterated the same conclusion regarding carcinogenicity risk assessment in Modules 2.4 and 2.6.6 of the BLA submission.
L1601: The Applicant proposed the following language for this section on 7/31/2020 (SDN 1):
L1602: *"The carcinogenic and genotoxic potential of anifrolumab-xxxx have not been evaluated".*
L1603: In a nonclinical information request (IR) dated 05/07/2021 the Applicant was asked to provide appropriate labeling to address the potential carcinogenic risk, as discussed above. The Applicant provided updated language for this section on 5/14/2021 (SDN 32). They proposed to add the following language:
L1604: *"In rodent models of IFNAR1 blockade, increased carcinogenic potential has been observed. The clinical relevance of these findings is unknown".*
L1605: A request for an evaluation of the proposed labeling for Section 13.1 was made by email on 06/08/2021 to the Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee (Exec CAC). The Exec CAC considered the above language sufficient to reveal carcinogenic potential.
L1606: In addition, the Applicant estimated that the exposures in cynomolgus monkeys receiving 9-month repeat dosing without drug-related adverse effects on male or female fertility was up to approximately (b)(4) times the exposure at the MRHD on an AUC basis. Since the AUC0-7d for the NOAEL of 50 mg/kg/week was 21600 µg.d/mL, and the highest mean AUC0-28d in patients with SLE receiving repeat IV dosing of 300 mg anifrolumab-fnia (once every 4 weeks for 52 weeks) was 1472 µg.d/mL between Weeks 48 to 52 for low Type 1 IFN gene signature subjects, the safety margin was calculated
L1607: to be around 58 (= 21600 \times 4 / 1472). A factor of 4 is applied to match the duration of AUC values before the comparison, i.e., 7 days vs 28 days.

L1618: ## 1 Executive Summary
L1619: ## 1.1 Introduction
L1620: AstraZeneca submitted a 351(a) Biologics License Application (BLA) on July 22, 2020, for Saphnelo™ (anifrolumab, MEDI-546) a proposed type I interferon receptor subunit 1 (IFNAR1) antagonist for the treatment of adult patients with moderate to severe systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE), (b)(4) The extractables and leachables study reports were submitted on January 8, 2021 and March 19, 2021.
L1621: (b)(4)
L1622: The anifrolumab Drug Substance (DS) is stored
L1623: This review is a nonclinical safety evaluation of potential extractables and leachables for the primary CCSs and in-process materials used in the DS and DP manufacture processes. Elemental analysis for the DP during stability study is also reviewed.
L1624: ## 1.2 Brief Discussion of Nonclinical Findings
L1625: (b)(4)
L1626: For the safety evaluation

L1632: ## 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L1633: This review provides a safety assessment of potential leachables [REDACTED] (b) (4) for DS storage, and the glass vial for the DP, based on data submitted by the sponsor and other available information. The sponsor is seeking approval for the DP at
L1634: a dose of 300 mg of anifrolumab Q4W via IV infusion. The sponsor established the TTC of (b)(4) \mug/day for nonmetals with genotoxic potential based on ICH M7 (2018). This correlated to an AET of (b)(4) \mug/mL for the dose volume of 2 mL/day. For metals, the ICH Q3D (2014) was followed for safety assessment.
L1635: Critical components from these CCSs, identified as the (b)(4) and the glass vial and stopper, were subjected to extractables and leachables studies. No leachables were identified above the TTC. For in-process materials used during the manufacture processes, no leachables were identified above the TTC. Also, based on the ongoing stability study for 3 lots of process validation products, no elemental impurities have been identified with safety concern.
L1636: Overall, there appear to be no nonclinical safety concerns for leachables from the DP.

L1641: Figure 1 displays two graphs showing model-predicted concentration-time profiles of anifrolumab after a single IV administration, comparing Type I IFN test high (left) and Type I IFN test low (right) status. The Y-axis represents Anifrolumab conc. (\mug/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 100). The X-axis represents Day (0 to 84 or 112).
Legend (Dose levels): 100 mg (Red solid line), 150 mg (Green dashed line), 300 mg (Blue dashed line), 1000 mg (Cyan dashed line).
Type I IFN test high: Shows rapid clearance. The 1000 mg dose reaches the lowest trough concentration (around 0.1 \mug/mL) by Day 28. The 100 mg dose reaches a trough around 0.5 \mug/mL.
Type I IFN test low: Shows slower clearance. The 1000 mg dose reaches the lowest trough concentration (around 0.1 \mug/mL) by Day 28. The 100 mg dose reaches a trough around 0.5 \mug/mL.
L1642: Figure 2 displays two graphs showing the Mean predose anifrolumab serum concentration-time profiles (MEDI-546 Concentration in \mug/mL vs. Elapsed Time in Days) for Study 1013, comparing 300 mg and 1000 mg treatment groups, stratified by baseline Type I IFN gene signature (IFN low vs. IFN high).

The legend indicates:
IFN low (Red circles)
IFN high (Green circles)
LLOQ (0.02 \mug/mL) (Dashed line)
300 mg (Left Graph): Concentrations are generally low (below 10 \mug/mL) for both IFN low and IFN high groups. The IFN low group shows a peak concentration around Day 336 (approx. 10 \mug/mL) followed by a rapid decline. The IFN high group maintains concentrations below 10 \mug/mL throughout the study.
1000 mg (Right Graph): Concentrations are significantly higher. The IFN low group shows a peak concentration around Day 336 (approx. 100 \mug/mL) followed by a rapid decline. The IFN high group maintains concentrations between 10 and 100 \mug/mL throughout the study, showing a steady decline phase after Day 336.
Error bars represent standard deviation of the mean. Arrows represent dosing. Dashed lines represent elimination phase.
L1643: Figure 3 is a line graph showing the Median % Type I IFN PD signature as percent of baseline in IFNGS high patients over 52 weeks for various studies and doses. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 100, and the X-axis represents Week (0 to 52).
Legend:
Study 05: 150 mg (Blue dashed line, circles)
Study 05: 300 mg (Orange solid line, triangles)
Study 04: 300 mg (Red dotted line, plus signs)
Study 1013: 300 mg (Gray dashed line, dots)
Study 1013: 1000 mg (Green dashed line, squares)
The graph illustrates that all treatment groups significantly reduce the Type I IFN gene signature compared to baseline (100%) by Week 4. The 1000 mg dose (Study 1013) shows the most rapid and substantial reduction, reaching approximately 10% of baseline by Week 4. The 150 mg dose (Study 05) shows a slower reduction, reaching approximately 70% of baseline by Week 52. The 300 mg doses (Studies 04 and 1013) show intermediate reductions, stabilizing around 10-15% of baseline by Week 52.

The overall significance level is 2-sided \alpha=0.05.
The primary endpoint, SRI(4) at Week 52, is tested first. If significant, the remaining alpha is recycled to the secondary endpoints:
SRI(4) IFN-high: Weight 0.8
Steroid reduction: Weight 0.08
CLASI: Weight 0.08
SRI(4) at Week 24: Weight 0.02
Flare rate: Weight 0.02
The diagram shows the flow of alpha recycling from the primary endpoint test to the subsequent secondary endpoint tests.

BICLA IFN-high: 0.8
Steroid reduction: 0.06
CLASI: 0.06
Joints: 0.06
Flare rate: 0.02
Arrows indicate the flow of statistical testing, starting from the primary endpoint and proceeding sequentially through the secondary endpoints, with alpha recycling applied to the subsequent tests.

L1660: Diagram illustrating the Anifrolumab PK Model. The model shows two compartments: the central compartment (Ab) and the peripheral tissue compartment (Abp). Abp is involved in Synthesis & Recycling, exchanging with Ab via intercompartmental clearance Q. Ab is cleared by CLRES and interacts with R (free receptor concentration) to form Ab-R (anifrolumab-IFN-\alphaR1 complex). The interaction between Ab and R is governed by association rate constant k_{on} and dissociation rate constant k_{off}. Ab-R is cleared via internalization rate constant k_{int}, leading to Intracellular Catabolism. R is also cleared by degradation rate constant k_{deg}. Endogenous production of R is represented by k_{syn}.

L1676: Figure 36 is a plot showing the predicted probability of meeting SRI(4) criteria at week 52 versus Anifrolumab Cave concentration over 52 weeks (in \mu\text{g/mL}). The plot includes observed data points (Obs 150 mg, n=75; Obs 300 mg, n=298) and predicted curves based on Baseline SLEDAI (SLEDAI\ge10 and SLEDAI<10), along with their 95% Confidence Intervals (CI).
The X-axis ranges from 0 to 220 \mu\text{g/mL}. The Y-axis ranges from 0.0 to 1.0, representing the Probability to meet SRI(4) criteria in all patients.
Observed data points are shown for 150 mg (circles) and 300 mg (triangles). Predicted curves are shown for Baseline SLEDAI\ge10 (green solid line) and Baseline SLEDAI<10 (orange dashed line), with corresponding 95% CIs (shaded areas).
The predicted probability of meeting SRI(4) increases with Anifrolumab Cave concentration. At 150 mg, the predicted probability is approximately 0.65 for SLEDAI\ge10 and 0.45 for SLEDAI<10. At 300 mg, the predicted probability is approximately 0.75 for SLEDAI\ge10 and 0.55 for SLEDAI<10. At 1000 mg, the predicted probability is approximately 0.85 for SLEDAI\ge10 and 0.65 for SLEDAI<10.
